{"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_5","type":"command_execution","command":"/bin/zsh -lc 'cat src/cljc/dao/stream/waitset/cadence.cljc; cat src/clj/dao/stream/waitset/driver.clj'","aggregated_output":"(ns dao.stream.waitset.cadence\n  \"The pure idle curve for a wait-set owner: how long to sleep after a round\n   that moved nothing.\n\n   Cadence belongs to the runtime driving the interpreters — `dao.stream.md`,\n   *What it costs* — and this namespace is the whole decidable-as-data part\n   of it. `cadence-step` never calls `check`, reads no clock, and touches no\n   stream, so it is testable on every host with no fixture at all: the curve\n   is arithmetic on values the host supplied.\n\n   The host loop owns everything else. It computes `moved?` from its own\n   round — any woken entry, any progress a consumer step reports, or any\n   outstanding pending write, which must never wait out a backoff ceiling —\n   threads the returned `:cadence-state` into the next call, and sleeps for\n   `:sleep-ms` through its host's wake source (`dao.stream.waitset.driver`).\n   The right cadence for a REPL is not the right cadence for a serving\n   endpoint; neither is this namespace's to know.\"\n  )\n\n\n(defn init\n  \"The cadence state for `params`, a host-supplied map:\n\n     {:poll-ms 25 :backoff {:factor 2 :ceiling-ms 200}}\n\n   `:poll-ms` is the base interval a wake resets to; `:backoff` is optional\n   and climbs an idle round by `:factor` up to `:ceiling-ms`, where it stays.\n   A fresh state is armed at `:poll-ms`. Parameters are composition data, so\n   a defect in them is named here rather than silently rounded: a\n   non-positive `:poll-ms`; a `:backoff` missing its factor or ceiling; a\n   `:factor` that is not a number of at least 1 (a smaller factor would\n   shrink the interval toward a zero busy-loop); or a `:ceiling-ms` below\n   `:poll-ms` (the curve must never idle faster than its base) — all throw\n   before any host sleeps on them.\"\n  [{:keys [poll-ms backoff], :as params}]\n  (when-not (and (number? poll-ms) (pos? poll-ms))\n    (throw (ex-info \"cadence needs a positive :poll-ms\" {:params params})))\n  (doseq [k [:factor :ceiling-ms]]\n    (when (and backoff (not (contains? backoff k)))\n      (throw (ex-info \"a cadence :backoff needs :factor and :ceiling-ms\"\n                      {:params params}))))\n  (when backoff\n    (when-not (and (number? (:factor backoff)) (>= (:factor backoff) 1))\n      (throw (ex-info \"a cadence :backoff :factor must be a number of at least 1\"\n                      {:params params})))\n    (when-not (and (number? (:ceiling-ms backoff))\n                   (>= (:ceiling-ms backoff) poll-ms))\n      (throw (ex-info \"a cadence :backoff :ceiling-ms must be a number of at least :poll-ms\"\n                      {:params params}))))\n  (assoc params :current poll-ms))\n\n\n(defn cadence-step\n  \"One round of the idle curve. `moved?` resets it: the next sleep is\n   `:poll-ms` again, whatever it had climbed to. An idle round arms the\n   interval the curve is already at, then climbs the curve — `:current ×\n   :factor`, capped at `:ceiling-ms`, flat once there — so a fresh owner's\n   first idle sleep is `:poll-ms` itself, and one without a `:backoff`\n   stays at `:poll-ms` forever.\n\n   Returns `{:cadence-state … :sleep-ms …}`: the state is threaded into the\n   next call unchanged by the host, and `:sleep-ms` arms the host's sleep.\n   Neither the ceiling nor a reset is stored anywhere else — whether\n   anything moved is derived per round, never retained.\"\n  [cadence-state moved?]\n  (let [poll-ms (:poll-ms cadence-state)\n        current (or (:current cadence-state) poll-ms)\n        {:keys [factor ceiling-ms], :or {factor 1}, :as backoff} (:backoff cadence-state)\n        base (if moved? poll-ms current)\n        armed (if backoff (min ceiling-ms base) base)\n        next-current (cond\n                       moved? poll-ms\n                       (nil? backoff) armed\n                       :else (min ceiling-ms (* factor armed)))]\n    {:cadence-state (assoc cadence-state :current next-current)\n     :sleep-ms armed}))\n(ns dao.stream.waitset.driver\n  \"Host policy — the JVM wake source for a wait-set owner.\n\n   This namespace is sleep machinery and nothing else. It holds no\n   interpreter state — no waitset, no store, no results, no cadence — and\n   dispatches nothing. The owner is an ordinary `loop` on one thread with\n   the waitset, the store, and the cadence state as loop locals:\n\n     check → consume :woken → cadence-step → sleep! → recur\n\n   A wake source is a `LinkedBlockingQueue` carrying contentless wake\n   tokens only: `nudge!` offers one, and `sleep!` polls with the timeout and\n   drains any surplus, so repeated nudges coalesce into one wake. This is\n   `select` with the wakeup pipe; the sweep is the syscall.\"\n  (:import (java.util.concurrent LinkedBlockingQueue TimeUnit)))\n\n\n(defn make-wake\n  \"A wake source: a queue of contentless tokens (Booleans) and nothing\n   else. It is never handed a waitset, a store, or a result.\"\n  []\n  (LinkedBlockingQueue.))\n\n\n(defn nudge!\n  \"Offer one contentless wake token: ask the owner's current sleep to end\n   early. Never blocks, runs no owner code inline, and may be called from\n   any thread — a line producer, a signal handler, a composition that just\n   appended. Repeated nudges coalesce inside `sleep!`'s drain. A nudge when\n   the owner is not sleeping costs one token that the next `sleep!`\n   consumes immediately.\"\n  [wake]\n  (.offer ^LinkedBlockingQueue wake true)\n  nil)\n\n\n(defn sleep!\n  \"Park up to `sleep-ms` for one wake token. Returns true when a nudge\n   arrived — draining every surplus token first, so however many nudges\n   landed, this is the one wake they coalesce into — and false when the\n   timeout elapsed. `sleep-ms` is the caller's cadence answer; this\n   function adds nothing to it and knows nothing about the round it ends.\"\n  [wake sleep-ms]\n  (if (.poll ^LinkedBlockingQueue wake (long sleep-ms) TimeUnit/MILLISECONDS)\n    (do (loop []\n          (when (.poll ^LinkedBlockingQueue wake)\n            (recur)))\n        true)\n    false))\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}
{"type":"item.started","item":{"id":"item_6","type":"command_execution","command":"/bin/zsh -lc 'cat src/cljs/dao/stream/waitset/driver.cljs; cat src/cljd/dao/stream/waitset/driver.cljd'","aggregated_output":"","exit_code":null,"status":"in_progress"}}
{"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_6","type":"command_execution","command":"/bin/zsh -lc 'cat src/cljs/dao/stream/waitset/driver.cljs; cat src/cljd/dao/stream/waitset/driver.cljd'","aggregated_output":"(ns dao.stream.waitset.driver\n  \"Host policy — the Node (and browser) wake source for a wait-set owner.\n\n   There is no sleeping loop on this host, so a wake source arms exactly one\n   timer at a time: `arm!` cancels any armed timer and arms another at\n   `:sleep-ms`, and a firing calls the composition root's own tick — the\n   zero-argument function handed to `make-wake`, exactly as `yin.repl`'s\n   Node shell hands its step to a timer. That tick owns the state box and\n   performs check → consume `:woken` → cadence-step → `arm!`; the driver\n   never sees the waitset, the store, or any result, and passes the tick no\n   arguments — this is the bottom of the stack a host timer occupies, not a\n   disposition callback.\n\n   `nudge!` cancels the armed timer and schedules the same tick once on a\n   microtask; a pending flag coalesces repeated nudges and keeps a nudge\n   issued from inside the tick from re-entering it — the tick re-arms\n   itself before returning, so a swallowed in-tick nudge costs nothing\n   until the interval it armed. An idle composition costs one timer, zero\n   busy loops.\")\n\n\n(defn make-wake\n  \"A wake source over `tick`, the composition root's own zero-argument step.\n   The wake holds only sleep machinery — a timer id and a pending flag — and\n   no interpreter state.\"\n  [tick]\n  {:tick tick\n   :timer (volatile! nil)\n   :pending? (volatile! false)})\n\n\n(defn disarm!\n  \"Cancel the armed timer, if any, and schedule nothing: teardown for an\n   owner whose tick has ended.\"\n  [wake]\n  (when-let [t @(:timer wake)]\n    (js/clearTimeout t))\n  (vreset! (:timer wake) nil)\n  nil)\n\n\n(defn arm!\n  \"Cancel-and-rearm: after this call exactly one timer is armed, firing\n   `sleep-ms` from now. A nil `sleep-ms` arms the shortest timer the host\n   allows. Node timers are unref'ed where the host supports it, so an idle\n   owner never holds the process open.\"\n  [wake sleep-ms]\n  (disarm! wake)\n  (vreset! (:timer wake)\n           (let [t (js/setTimeout (fn []\n                                    (vreset! (:timer wake) nil)\n                                    ((:tick wake)))\n                                  (or sleep-ms 0))]\n             (when (fn? (.-unref t))\n               (.unref t))\n             t))\n  nil)\n\n\n(defn nudge!\n  \"End the current sleep early: cancel the armed timer and schedule the tick\n   once on a microtask. Never runs the tick inline in the caller. Repeated\n   nudges coalesce — the pending flag stays up until the tick it scheduled\n   has run — and a nudge issued while the flag is up (from inside a tick,\n   before or after that tick's own `arm!`) touches nothing: no disarm, no\n   schedule, so the timer the tick last armed is the next round and the\n   owner is never left with neither. The tick's own re-arm is the next\n   guaranteed round; a swallowed nudge costs at most the interval then\n   armed.\"\n  [wake]\n  (when-not @(:pending? wake)\n    (disarm! wake)\n    (vreset! (:pending? wake) true)\n    (js/queueMicrotask\n      (fn []\n        (try\n          ((:tick wake))\n          (finally\n            (vreset! (:pending? wake) false))))))\n  nil)\n(ns dao.stream.waitset.driver\n  \"Host policy — the Dart wake source for a wait-set owner.\n\n   Dart runs one isolate and has no sleeping loop, so a wake source arms\n   exactly one `Timer` at a time: `arm!` cancels any armed timer and arms\n   another at `:sleep-ms`, and a firing calls the composition root's own\n   tick — the zero-argument function handed to `make-wake`, exactly as\n   `yin.repl`'s Dart shell hands its step to `Timer.periodic`. That tick\n   owns the state box and performs check → consume `:woken` → cadence-step\n   → `arm!`; the driver never sees the waitset, the store, or any result,\n   and passes the tick no arguments — this is the bottom of the stack a\n   host timer occupies, not a disposition callback.\n\n   `nudge!` cancels the armed timer and schedules the same tick once on a\n   microtask; a pending flag coalesces repeated nudges and keeps a nudge\n   issued from inside the tick from re-entering it — the tick re-arms\n   itself before returning, so a swallowed in-tick nudge costs nothing\n   until the interval it armed. An idle composition costs one timer, zero\n   busy loops.\"\n  (:require [\"dart:async\" :as async]\n            [\"dart:core\" :as dart-core]))\n\n\n(defn make-wake\n  \"A wake source over `tick`, the composition root's own zero-argument step.\n   The wake holds only sleep machinery — a timer and a pending flag — and\n   no interpreter state.\"\n  [tick]\n  {:tick tick\n   :timer (volatile! nil)\n   :pending? (volatile! false)})\n\n\n(defn disarm!\n  \"Cancel the armed timer, if any, and schedule nothing: teardown for an\n   owner whose tick has ended.\"\n  [wake]\n  (when-let [t @(:timer wake)]\n    (.cancel ^async/Timer t))\n  (vreset! (:timer wake) nil)\n  nil)\n\n\n(defn arm!\n  \"Cancel-and-rearm: after this call exactly one timer is armed, firing\n   `sleep-ms` from now. A nil `sleep-ms` arms the shortest timer the host\n   allows.\"\n  [wake sleep-ms]\n  (disarm! wake)\n  (vreset! (:timer wake)\n           (async/Timer.\n             (dart-core/Duration .milliseconds (or sleep-ms 0))\n             (fn []\n               (vreset! (:timer wake) nil)\n               ((:tick wake)))))\n  nil)\n\n\n(defn nudge!\n  \"End the current sleep early: cancel the armed timer and schedule the tick\n   once on a microtask. Never runs the tick inline in the caller. Repeated\n   nudges coalesce — the pending flag stays up until the tick it scheduled\n   has run — and a nudge issued while the flag is up (from inside a tick,\n   before or after that tick's own `arm!`) touches nothing: no disarm, no\n   schedule, so the timer the tick last armed is the next round and the\n   owner is never left with neither. The tick's own re-arm is the next\n   guaranteed round; a swallowed nudge costs at most the interval then\n   armed.\"\n  [wake]\n  (when-not @(:pending? wake)\n    (disarm! wake)\n    (vreset! (:pending? wake) true)\n    (async/scheduleMicrotask\n      (fn []\n        (try\n          ((:tick wake))\n          (finally\n            (vreset! (:pending? wake) false))))))\n  nil)\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

Per host, a line producer\n-   appends what was typed to the input medium and returns; one ticker owns the\n-   state value and calls `repl-step` serially.  There is no second state owner,\n-   and no line handler evaluates, requests, polls, or prints.\n+   appends what was typed to the input medium, nudges the step owner's wake\n+   source, and returns; one ticker owns the state value and calls\n+   `repl-step` serially at the interval `cadence-step` computes.  There is\n+   no second state owner, and no line handler evaluates, requests, polls,\n+   or prints.\n \n    `yin.repl` is untouched and keeps its aliases; this is a second REPL beside\n    it.\"\n@@ -13,17 +15,30 @@\n                        [\"dart:convert\" :as convert]\n                        [\"dart:core\" :as dart-core]\n                        [\"dart:io\" :as io]])\n+            [dao.stream.waitset.cadence :as cadence]\n+            [dao.stream.waitset.driver :as wake]\n             [yin.repl.driver :as driver]\n             [yin.repl.host :as host]\n             [yin.repl.serve :as serve]))\n \n \n (def tick-millis\n-  \"Cadence of the single step owner.  Slow enough to cost nothing while idle,\n-   fast enough that a remote result prints without waiting for a keystroke.\"\n+  \"The base cadence of the single step owner.  Slow enough to cost nothing\n+   while anything moves, fast enough that a remote result prints without\n+   waiting for a keystroke.\"\n   25)\n \n \n+(def default-cadence\n+  \"The tick owner's idle curve.  `tick-millis` is the base interval — what a\n+   wake resets to and what a pending write holds — and an idle composition\n+   backs off doubling to 200 ms.  Local input and the explicit stop trigger\n+   nudge the wake source, so operator actions never wait the curve out; a\n+   lost nudge costs at most the armed interval.\"\n+  {:poll-ms tick-millis\n+   :backoff {:factor 2 :ceiling-ms 200}})\n+\n+\n (def prompt \"yin> \")\n \n \n@@ -110,6 +125,18 @@\n     [state'' server'' (mapv entry-text (into (vec entries) server-entries))]))\n \n \n+(defn moved?\n+  \"The tick owner's cadence bit, computed from this tick's own results: true\n+   when there are lines to print, when the endpoint reports movement — a\n+   woken probe, a published notice — or when either composition still owes a\n+   write.  A pending write keeps cadence at the base interval; it must never\n+   wait out a backoff ceiling.\"\n+  [state server lines]\n+  (boolean (or (seq lines)\n+               (driver/pending-write? state)\n+               (and server (serve/moved? server)))))\n+\n+\n ;; =============================================================================\n ;; Shutdown\n ;; =============================================================================\n@@ -142,9 +169,15 @@\n    This is the whole of what a host signal handler, a headless supervisor, or a\n    test does to stop the composition. (:input state) \"(quit)\"))\n+  ([state w]\n+   (let [result (request-stop! state)]\n+     (when w (wake/nudge! w))\n+     result)))\n \n \n (defn stop-tick\n@@ -176,8 +209,9 @@\n        \"The shell has quit, so the endpoint stops before the host exits: ask it\n         once, then keep stepping until it reports `:stopped` or the bounded\n         budget runs out. Returns the wake so the composition can wire its\n+        line producers — the readline handlers, the stop signals — as\n+        `nudge!` callers.\n+\n+        `repl-step` is synchronous and the Node event loop is single\n+        threaded, so a tick cannot overlap itself. stopping)\n   467\t                        (let [[state' server' lines] (step-all state server now)]\n   468\t                          (reset! box {:state state'\n   469\t                                       :server server'\n   470\t                                       :stopping nil\n   471\t                                       :cadence cadence})\n   472\t                          (doseq [line lines]\n   473\t                            (write-line! line))\n   474\t                          (cond\n   475\t                            (:running? state')\n   476\t                            (let [{:keys [cadence-state sleep-ms]}\n   477\t                                  (cadence/cadence-step cadence\n   478\t                                                        (moved? state' server' lines))]\n   479\t                              (swap! box assoc :cadence cadence-state)\n   480\t                              (when (and (seq lines) (not headless?))\n   481\t                                (print-prompt!))\n   482\t                              (wake/arm! @wake-ref sleep-ms))\n   483\t\n   484\t                            server'\n   485\t                            (do (swap! box assoc\n   486\t                                       :server (serve/stop! server')\n   487\t                                       :stopping stop-ticks)\n   488\t                                (wake/arm! @wake-ref tick-millis)\n   489\t                                nil)\n   490\t\n   491\t                            :else (finish!)))\n   492\t                        (let [[server' lines stopped?] (stop-tick server now)]\n   493\t                          (doseq [line lines]\n   494\t                            (write-line! line))\n   495\t                          (cond\n   496\t                            stopped? (boolean (:headless? opts))]\n   514\t         (doseq [line (banner opts)]\n   515\t           (write-line! line))\n   516\t         ;; The tick owner arms its first round, and the composition wires the\n   517\t         ;; deposit it hands each line producer with the nudge: a typed line\n   518\t         ;; ends the idle sleep at once. (:input state) \"(quit)\")\n   535\t                                        (wake/nudge! w))))\n   665\t\n   666\t\n   667\t(defn- session-step\n   668\t  \"Advance one session one bounded step.  A session holding a pending\n   669\t   response is retried every round the host runs — its wait is host cadence,\n   670\t   never a probe, and while it holds one no probe is parked (one active\n   671\t   waiter per session). A session with nothing pending\n   674\t   and no wake keeps its probe parked — no unconditional read remains.\"\n   675\t  [endpoint attachment selected?]\n   676\t  (let [session (get-in endpoint [:sessions attachment])]\n   677\t    (cond\n   678\t      (or (nil? session) (:terminal session) (nil? (:cursor session))) endpoint\n   679\t\n   680\t      (:pending-response session) (deliver-response endpoint attachment)\n   681\t\n   682\t      selected?\n   683\t      (let [result (stream/next (:reader session) (:cursor session))\n   684\t            outcome (:dao.stream/outcome result)]\n   685\t        (case outcome\n   686\t          :dao.stream/ok\n   687\t          (interpret-element endpoint attachment\n   688\t                             (:dao.stream/value result)\n   689\t                             (:dao.stream/cursor result))\n   690\t\n   691\t          :dao.stream/blocked endpoint\n   692\t\n   693\t          ;; A defective peer can still overrun its own request medium.  The\n   694\t          ;; session is closed rather than left with an unanswerable request.\n   695\t          :dao.stream/gap\n   696\t          (-> endpoint\n   697\t              (assoc-in [:sessions attachment :cursor] (:dao.stream/cursor result))\n   698\t              (publish :yin.repl.serve/notice\n   699\t                       (str \";; requests lost from \" attachment \"; closing it\"))\n   700\t              (close-attachment! attachment :dao.stream/gap))\n   701\t\n   702\t          (close-attachment! endpoint attachment outcome)))\n   703\t\n   704\t      :else endpoint)))\n   705\t\n   706\t\n   707\t(defn- advance-sessions\n   708\t  \"One bounded step per session, in `:sessions` keys order — the same order\n   709\t   every prior form of this driver reduced in.  Pending responses first —\n   710\t   their retry runs on the host's cadence, gated on nothing — then the\n   711\t   sessions this sweep's probes woke; the rest keep their probes parked.\"\n   712\t  [endpoint selected]\n   713\t  (reduce (fn [endpoint attachment]\n   714\t            (session-step endpoint attachment (contains? selected attachment)))\n   715\t          endpoint\n   716\t          (keys (:sessions endpoint))))\n   717\t\n   718\t\n   719\t;; =============================================================================\n   720\t;; The driver step\n   721\t;; =============================================================================\n   722\t\n   723\t(defn stopped?\n   724\t  \"True when nothing more is owed to this endpoint's shutdown: it reported\n   725\t   `:stopped`, or it never composed a serving driver at all.\n   726\t\n   727\t   A refused bind configuration or a missing host package leaves an endpoint\n   728\t   that owns its media and its reason and nothing else.  No host close\n   729\t   completion exists to deposit `:stopped` for it, so a shutdown that waited for\n   730\t   one would spend its whole budget and then report a timeout for a listener\n   731\t   that never bound.\"\n   732\t  [endpoint]\n   733\t  (or (nil? endpoint)\n   734\t      (= :stopped (:status endpoint))\n   735\t      (nil? (:serving endpoint))))\n   736\t\n   737\t\n   738\t(defn stop!\n   739\t  \"Initiate stop, claiming nothing about completion.\n   740\t\n   741\t   Closing the service stream is what makes a connected client deposit\n   742\t   `:ws/ended` rather than `:ws/closed`; the serving composition performs that\n   743\t   transport-specific close on its next step.  Only the host close completion\n   744\t   deposits `:stopped`, and only `step` consuming it marks the stop complete and\n   745\t   releases the resolution-table entry.\n   746\t\n   747\t   An endpoint with no serving driver is left exactly as it is: it holds no\n   748\t   listener and no attachment, so there is nothing to initiate, and marking it\n   749\t   `:stopping` would both claim a stop nobody can complete and erase the status\n   750\t   that says why it never started.\"\n   751\t  [endpoint]\n   752\t  (if (or (contains? #{:stopping :stopped} (:status endpoint))\n   753\t          (nil? (:serving endpoint)))\n   754\t    endpoint\n   755\t    (do\n   756\t      (when (:service endpoint)\n   757\t        (stream/close! (:service endpoint)))\n   758\t      (assoc endpoint :status :stopping))))\n   759\t\n   760\t\n   761\t(defn- finish-stop\n   762\t  [endpoint]\n   763\t  (if (and (= :stopping (:status endpoint))\n   764\t           (not (:stop-initiated? endpoint))\n   765\t           (:serving endpoint))\n   766\t    (let [result (serving/stop! (:serving endpoint))\n   767\t          endpoint (assoc endpoint :stop-initiated? true)]\n   768\t      (if (= :dao.stream/transport-error (:dao.stream/outcome result))\n   769\t        ;; A synchronous release failure has no later host completion to\n   770\t        ;; observe.  The composition owns that fact and resolves locally,\n   771\t        ;; retaining the structured reason in the ordinary notice stream.\n   772\t        (-> endpoint\n   773\t            (assoc :status :stopped :resolution nil)\n   774\t            (publish :yin.repl.serve/notice\n   775\t                     (str \"Endpoint stopped without host completion: \"\n   776\t                          (pr-str (data/summarize (:dao.stream/diagnostic result) diagnostic-bounds)))))\n   777\t        endpoint))\n   778\t    endpoint))\n   779\t\n   780\t\n   781\t(defn step\n   782\t  \"Advance the endpoint once at `now`, returning the next endpoint value.\n   783\t\n   784\t   Ordering is intentional: lifecycle first, so a bind result is known before\n   785\t   anything claims to be serving; then the transport and serving composition;\n   786\t   then session adoption, parking each new session's request-medium probe;\n   787\t   then one sweep of the probe wait set, whose wakes select the sessions\n   788\t   that read this step; then one bounded step per session — a pending\n   789\t   response first, a selected session's own re-read — against the single\n   790\t   shared REPL state; then probe maintenance, so a session holds exactly one\n   791\t   active waiter.\"\n   792\t  [endpoint now]\n   793\t  (if-not endpoint\n   794\t    endpoint\n   795\t    (let [outbox-before (count (:outbox endpoint))\n   796\t          endpoint (drain-lifecycle endpoint)]\n   797\t      (if-not (:serving endpoint)\n   798\t        ;; An endpoint that never composed a serving driver — a refused bind\n   799\t        ;; configuration or a missing host package — still owns and reports its\n   800\t        ;; lifecycle medium; it simply has no sessions to advance.\n   801\t        endpoint\n   802\t        (do\n   803\t          (serving/step! (:serving endpoint) now)\n   804\t          (let [endpoint (sync-sessions endpoint)\n   805\t                {:keys [woken], :as result}\n   806\t                (waitset/check (:probes endpoint) probe-resolver endpoint)\n   807\t                endpoint (assoc endpoint :probes (:waitset result))\n   808\t                selected (set (map #(:attachment (:entry %)) woken))\n   809\t                endpoint (advance-sessions endpoint selected)]\n   810\t            (-> endpoint\n   811\t                maintain-probes\n   812\t                (assoc :step-moved? (boolean (or (seq woken)\n   813\t                                                 (> (count (:outbox endpoint))\n   814\t                                                    outbox-before))))\n   815\t                finish-stop)))))))\n   816\t\n   817\t\n   818\t(defn moved?\n   819\t  \"True when the last `step` moved something a caller's cadence must not\n   820\t   sleep through: a probe woke, or a notice was published — or a session\n   821\t   still owes a response append, which must never wait out a backoff\n   822\t   ceiling.\"\n   823\t  [endpoint]\n   824\t  (boolean (or (:step-moved? endpoint)\n   825\t               (some (fn [session]\n   826\t                       (or (:pending-response session)\n   827\t                           (:pending-successor session)))\n   828\t                     (vals (:sessions endpoint))))))\n   829\t\n   830\t\n   831\t(defn summary\n   832\t  \"A serializable summary of endpoint state, for `(repl-state)` and tests.\"\n   833\t  [endpoint]\n   834\t  (when endpoint\n   835\t    {:status (:status endpoint)\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

{"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_9","type":"command_execution","command":"/bin/zsh -lc 'cat collab/1789852500000-reviewer-waitset-w34-r2.claude-fable-5-1.findings.md; cat collab/1789851200000-reviewer-waitset-w34.claude-fable-5-1.findings.md'","aggregated_output":"Completed-GMT: 2026-09-20 09:56:37 GMT\nCompleted-Local: 2026-09-20 16:56:37 +07\n\n# W3+W4 r2 confirmation\n\n**W3+W4 is ready for architect sign-off.** Both P2 liveness fixes are correct, and I found no new defect in the touched lines or the new tests. One P3 has no code or test part left, only a missing plan sentence: the latency note is not in this tree's plan. I edited nothing and did not rerun suites.\n\n| Finding | Disposition | Evidence | Remaining action |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| P2 throwing tick kills the owner | **Fixed** | Both ticks arm a fallback at `tick-millis` before the body runs, and the body's own `arm!` replaces it (`repl.cljc`, `run-node!` and `run-dart!`). This is the alternative fix I offered, in place of the `try`/`finally`. `finish!` disarms, so a finished owner leaves nothing armed. A tick that keeps throwing retries from the same state, because the box is not written before the throw. The old `setInterval` behaved the same way. `a-tick-whose-body-fails-before-its-own-arm-still-ticks-again` is present on cljs and cljd. | None. |\n| P2 `nudge!` disarmed before the pending check | **Fixed** | `disarm!` now sits inside `when-not @(:pending? wake)` on both hosts. `a-nudge-from-inside-a-tick-leaves-the-timer-it-armed-alone` uses a tick that calls `arm!` and then `nudge!`. It requires at least three ticks, and the old ordering would have stranded it at one. A timer can never fire before a queued microtask, so skipping the disarm while the flag is up is safe. | None. |\n| P3 cadence validation | **Fixed** | `init` refuses a `:factor` that is not a number of at least 1, and a `:ceiling-ms` below `:poll-ms`. | None. |\n| P3 nil-cursor probe spin | **Fixed** | `(some? The tests simulate the state after the throw and do not throw.\nCompleted-GMT: 2026-09-20 09:35:31 GMT\nCompleted-Local: 2026-09-20 16:35:31 +07\n\n# Adversarial review: dao.stream.waitset Phases W3+W4\n\n**W3+W4 is not yet ready for architect sign-off.** Two P2 liveness defects in the cljs and cljd wake sources should be fixed first. Both fixes are a few lines. I found no P0 or P1, and everything else conforms to the plan. I edited nothing and did not rerun suites.\n\n## What holds\n\n- **Pure layer.** `cadence.cljc` requires nothing, reads no clock and calls no `check`. A wake resets the curve to `:poll-ms`, and an idle owner climbs to the ceiling and stays there.\n- **Wake sources.**\n  - Host state is a queue of contentless tokens on clj, and a timer plus a pending flag on cljs and cljd.\n  - The host files contain no waitset, store or results.\n  - They have no `make-driver` and no budget.\n  - The tick receives no arguments.\n- **Nudge on the JVM.** `sleep!` drains surplus tokens, so repeated nudges coalesce. `nudge!` never blocks and runs no owner code inline.\n- **Deletions.** A grep for `setInterval`, `Timer.periodic` and `Thread/sleep` across `yin/repl.cljc` and `yin/repl/` returns nothing. The `advance-sessions` call that read every session unconditionally is gone: `session-step` reads only when `selected?` is true.\n- **Substrate.** No file under `src/cljc/dao/stream*` is modified. The only additions are `cadence.cljc` and the three `driver` files.\n- **Serve assignment, row by row.**\n  - **One waiter per session.** The resolver refuses a terminal or pending session. `maintain-probes` retires probes and parks new ones after each step.\n  - **Pending responses.** `deliver-response` runs first in `session-step`, and it is not gated on the probe.\n  - **Probe advance.** The probe's `:advance` is identity. `session-step` re-reads the medium and stays the only commit authority.\n  - **Non-`ok` wakes.** A probe that wakes with a status other than `ok` still selects its session, so the session's own step applies its own gap and close policy.\n  - **Departed sessions.** Their probes are retired in `sync-sessions`, not by polling.\n- **Unchanged rows.** `serving/step!` and the ws acknowledgement sweep are untouched. `remote.cljc` swaps `Thread/sleep` for `sleep!` inside the `:cljd []`-first splice and gains nothing else.\n- **Judgment call 1 (`serve/moved?` counts a round where a probe woke).** It is sound. A wake means a request was read. The round that delivers the response is not counted, so the next sleep is still the base interval.\n- **Judgment call 2 (shutdown drain at the flat base interval).** It is sound. The drain is a bounded budget of `stop-ticks`, and a backoff curve there would only lengthen shutdown.\n\n## Findings\n\n**P2 | `src/cljs/dao/stream/waitset/driver.cljs:36-49` and `.cljd` `arm!` | A tick that throws kills the owner for good.**\n- The timer callback clears `:timer`, runs the tick, and relies on the tick calling `arm!` as its last act.\n- If `step-all` throws, nothing re-arms. The old `setInterval` and `Timer.periodic` ticks fired again at the next interval.\n- The shell then hangs silently on Node and Dart. JVM behaviour is unchanged, because the poller thread already died on a throw.\n- Fix: in the `run-node!` and `run-dart!` ticks, wrap the body in `try`/`finally`. If no timer is armed and the owner has not finished, the `finally` arms `tick-millis`. Alternatively, arm a fallback at the start of the tick and let the final `arm!` replace it. Add a test with a tick that throws once and is still called again.\n\n**P2 | `driver.cljs:63-72` and `driver.cljd` `nudge!` | `nudge!` disarms the timer before it checks the pending flag.**\n- A nudge issued inside a nudge-scheduled tick, after that tick's `arm!`, cancels the timer the tick just armed. It then schedules nothing because `pending?` is true.\n- The result is no timer and no microtask, so the owner is dead until an outside nudge arrives.\n- The docstring says a swallowed in-tick nudge \"costs nothing\", which is true only when the nudge comes before the `arm!`.\n- The current shell ticks arm last and never nudge themselves, so the hazard is latent. A composition that wraps its deposit with a nudge and deposits from inside its own tick would hit it.\n- `a-nudge-from-inside-a-tick-does-not-re-enter-it` uses a tick that never arms, so it cannot see this.\n- Fix: move `(disarm! wake)` inside the `when-not @(:pending? wake)` branch. Add a test where the tick calls `arm!` then `nudge!`, and a later timer tick still fires.\n\n**P3 | `cadence.cljc` `init` | Validation checks that `:factor` and `:ceiling-ms` exist, not what they are.**\n- A `:factor` below 1 shrinks the interval toward zero. On the JVM `(long 0.x)` is 0, so `sleep!` polls with a zero timeout and spins. A `:factor` of exactly 0 zeroes it in one step. That contradicts \"zero busy loops\".\n- A ceiling below `:poll-ms` silently sleeps less than the base interval.\n- The docstring promises that defects are \"named here rather than silently rounded\".\n- Fix: require a numeric `:factor` of at least 1 and a `:ceiling-ms` of at least `:poll-ms`. Extend `parameters-are-validated-at-init` to cover both.\n\n**P3 | `serve.cljc` `probe-resolver` and `maintain-probes` | A session adopted with a nil cursor spins its probe.**\n- `adopt` stores `(mint …)`, which is nil when the cursor mint fails. `session-step` skips such a session.\n- Its probe resolves with a nil cursor and wakes terminally. The session is selected and skipped, then `maintain-probes` parks a fresh probe.\n- This repeats every round. `:step-moved?` is true every round, so the endpoint's cadence is pinned at 25 ms indefinitely.\n- The old code skipped such a session quietly.\n- Fix: add `(some? (:cursor session))` to `observing?` and to the resolver's guard.\n\n**P3 | `repl.cljc` `default-cadence` | The idle endpoint now answers the first request up to 200 ms late, where it used to be 25 ms.**\n- Only the line producers and `request-stop!` call `nudge!`. The serving inbound path does not.\n- The plan lists exactly those nudge callers and accepts \"a lost nudge costs the armed interval\", so this conforms.\n- The latency change should be recorded. Alternatively the ceiling could be lowered for a served composition.\n\n**P3 | `serve.cljc` `advance-sessions` docstring | It says sessions step \"in wait-set order\".**\n- The reduce runs over `(keys (:sessions endpoint))`, the same order as before. Correct the sentence.\n\n**P3 | Test gap against the W4 deliverable.**\n- The deliverable asks for a test that \"no probe shares cursor state with an advancing entry\".\n- `a-probe-carries-no-cursor-state-and-wakes-on-the-current-position` shows that the probe entry carries only `:reason` and `:attachment`. That is the structural half.\n- In serve, probe rule 3 (cursor-state isolation) holds trivially because the wait set contains only probes. One assertion that every entry in `:probes` has an identity `:advance`, or a comment saying so, would close the item.\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}
{"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_10","type":"agent_message","text":"The W3/W4 implementation satisfies the agreed architecture.\n\nThe two-layer split is sound. `cadence-step` is host-independent and has no clock, stream, waitset, or interpreter access. The host drivers contain only wake machinery: JVM contentless queue tokens; CLJS/CLJD timer and pending-flag state. The composition owns the waitset, store, cadence state, and tick. The retained tick is the composition root’s execution entry, not a waitset disposition callback; the driver never receives `:woken` or interpreter state.\n\nThe fallback-arm fix is sound. Each CLJS/CLJD tick arms a fallback before executing its body, then the normal body arm replaces it. A throw therefore leaves a future tick armed from the same state because state is written only after the step succeeds. `finish!` disarms the source, so completed owners do not continue scheduling. As the reviewer noted, an uncaught exception can still terminate the host process; the fallback protects the owner when the host continues running, which is the same operational boundary as the prior timer owner.\n\nW4’s deletions satisfy the plan:\n\n- The shell’s unconditional fixed 25 ms cadence is replaced by `cadence-step` plus host sleep/wake.\n- `yin.repl.serve` removes the unconditional per-session read and uses one probe or pending-response retry per session.\n- Pending writes remain on host cadence and are never represented as external `:put` waiters.\n- `dao.stream.serving/step!` remains host cadence because it has mandatory endpoint work, already polls each medium once, and would gain duplicate reads without deleting the work it replaces.\n- The remote daemon changes only its sleep primitive; its host-owned serving step remains intact.\n\nThere is no architectural objection to recording the served-first-request latency rather than lowering the shared ceiling. The consequence is real: without a serving-path nudge, an idle endpoint may wait up to the configured 200 ms ceiling before its probe runs. Recording it is the correct composition-level tradeoff; a serving-specific cadence or nudge can be chosen later without changing the waitset contract.\n\nThe four P3 follow-ups remain non-blocking. The provided test results cover the fixed liveness paths and all three hosts.\n\nSIGN-OFF: granted — W3 and W4 preserve exclusive host ownership, correct fallback liveness, and the required adoption deletions, with the documented latency tradeoff accepted as composition policy."}}
